4.2
Coarse-graining of loop holonomy operator

One of the goals of this part is to show that the action of loop holonomy operator on a graph can be exactly mapped to the action on its coarse-grained graph. We present the transformation rule for the two actions. Based on that, we show that the dynamics of spin network entanglement can be exactly studied from the coarse-grained graph.

Coarse-graining the loop
W
is done by keeping the edges that are not coarse-grained, then by gluing them into loop to be referred to
W_{(R)}
, e.g. Fig.
6
.

Two trivial situations are not to be considered: (i)
W
is completely isolated in certain
\Gamma_{i}
. (ii) Trivial coarse-graining
W_{(R)}=W
.

According to Lemma
4.1
, the evolution on these loopy spin sub-networks
\{\Upsilon_{i}\}
is determined by the bouquet spins and the spins on the loop edges. So the transition matrix
Z
can be obtained from studying the type of particular graphs that the bouquet spins are represented by boundary spins, such as Fig.
6
.

Now consider two evolutions: (a) Evolution
|\psi_{\Gamma}\rangle\to|\psi_{\Gamma}(t)\rangle
generated by loop holonomy operator acting on
W\subset\Gamma
. (b) Evolution
|\psi_{\Gamma_{(R)}}\rangle\to|\psi_{\Gamma_{(R)}}(t)\rangle
generated by loop holonomy operator action on
W_{(R)}\subset\Gamma_{(R)}
.
Then we arrive the following result:

.

Suppose the oriented loop
W[v_{1}\overset{e_{1}}{\to}\cdots\overset{e_{p-1}}{\to}v_{p}\overset{e_{p}}{\to}v_{1}]
is partitioned by
n
sub-networks
\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, i.e.
W=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
where
W_{i}\subset\Gamma_{i}
.
For every
\Gamma_{i}
, we relabel the bouquet spins
j_{\alpha}
and bulk spins
k_{\alpha}
along the
W_{i}
and denote them by

\{j^{(i)}_{\alpha}\}\equiv\{j^{(i)}_{1}\,,\cdots\,,j^{(i)}_{p_{i}}\}\,,\qquad\{k_{\alpha}^{(i)}\}\equiv\{k^{(i)}_{0}\,,k^{(i)}_{1}\,,\cdots\,,k^{(i)}_{p_{i}}\}=\{k_{\alpha}^{o(i)}\}\sqcup\{k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}

with the implicit convention
k^{(i)}_{p_{i}}=k^{(i+1)}_{0}
due to the spin-matching constraint, where

\{k_{\alpha}^{o(i)}\}\equiv\{k^{(i)}_{1}\,,\cdots\,,k^{(i)}_{p_{i}-1}\}\,,\qquad\{k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}\equiv\{k^{(i)}_{0}\,,k^{(i)}_{p_{i}}\}\,,

because
\{k_{\alpha}^{o(i)}\}
are bulk spins for
\Gamma_{i}
, and
\{k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}
are part of boundary spins for
\Gamma_{i}
. Spins
\{j_{\alpha}^{(i)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}
define cluster intertwiners

I_{u_{i}}\in\mathrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big[{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{0}^{(i)}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{p_{i}}^{(i)}}\otimes\ \bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{p_{i}}{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{\alpha}}\Big]\,.

(71)

Now the cluster intertwiner is labeled by
\{j_{\alpha}^{(i)},k_{\alpha}^{(i)}\}
, and recoupling spins
\{j_{\alpha}^{(i)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}
leads to the representation

for channel transformation:

(74)

\displaystyle\phantom{\sum_{\{I_{u_{i}}^{o}\}}\sum_{\{\widetilde{I}_{u_{i}}^{o}\}}}\mathchoice{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Gamma_{(R)}}\,\Big]}^{{{\{I_{u_{i}}\}}\mathchoice{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[21.84259pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[20.0241pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.7479pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[16.02261pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Gamma_{(R)}}\,\Big]}^{{{\{I_{u_{i}}\}}\mathchoice{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[21.84259pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[20.0241pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.7479pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[16.02261pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Gamma_{(R)}}\,\Big]}^{{{\{I_{u_{i}}\}}\mathchoice{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[21.84259pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[20.0241pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.7479pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[16.02261pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Gamma_{(R)}}\,\Big]}^{{{\{I_{u_{i}}\}}\mathchoice{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[29.03247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[21.84259pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[20.0241pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.09908pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[16.7479pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[16.02261pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}}}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{p}\delta_{J_{\alpha}j_{\alpha}}\,,

where
I_{u_{i}}=\{j_{\alpha}^{(i)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}\cup I_{u_{i}}^{o}
and
\widetilde{I}_{u_{i}}=\{J_{\alpha}^{(i)},K_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}\cup\widetilde{I}_{u_{i}}^{o}
, and the transition matrix

Figure 7
:
The illustration for recoupling spins to acquire cluster intertwiner
I_{u_{i}}
.

Proof.

The transition matrix
Z
eq.(
70
) can be computed from the type of graph
\Gamma=W\sqcup{\mathcal{E}}^{\partial}
where
{\mathcal{E}}^{\partial}=\partial\Gamma
is the set of
p
boundary edges
\{e^{\partial}_{1},\cdots,e^{\partial}_{p}\}
, and
\Gamma^{o}=W
.
Let us consider the simplest case
n=2
. The proof is straightforward to generalize to arbitrary
n
.
Suppose bipartition
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\sqcup\Gamma_{2}
,

\Gamma_{1}=T_{1}\sqcup\{e^{\partial}_{1},\cdots,e^{\partial}_{q},e_{p}^{(1)},e_{q}^{(1)}\}\,,\qquad\Gamma_{2}=T_{2}\sqcup\{e^{\partial}_{q+1},\cdots,e^{\partial}_{p},e_{p}^{(2)},e_{q}^{(2)}\}\,.

(76)

where
e_{p}^{(1)}
,
e_{q}^{(1)}
,
e_{p}^{(2)}
,
e_{q}^{(2)}
are due to the partition that splits edges
e_{p}
and
e_{q}
with
e_{p}=e_{p}^{(1)}\sqcup e_{p}^{(2)}
and
e_{q}=e_{q}^{(1)}\sqcup e_{q}^{(2)}
.
The bulks of
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
are
\Gamma_{1}^{o}=T_{1}[v_{1}\overset{e_{1}}{\to}\cdots\overset{e_{q-2}}{\to}v_{q-1}\overset{e_{q-1}}{\to}v_{q}]
and
\Gamma_{2}^{o}=T_{2}[v_{q+1}\overset{e_{q+1}}{\to}\cdots\overset{e_{p-2}}{\to}v_{p-1}\overset{e_{p-1}}{\to}v_{p}]
, respectively. In particular,
W_{1}=T_{1}\sqcup\{e_{p}^{(1)},e_{q}^{(1)}\}
and
W_{2}=T_{2}\sqcup\{e_{p}^{(2)},e_{q}^{(2)}\}
so
W=W_{1}\sqcup W_{2}
.
An example is illustrated by Fig.
6
.
We start by noticing

(77)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\prod_{e\in W}\mathrm{d}g_{e}\,\underline{\langle\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{J_{\alpha},K_{\alpha}\}}(\{g_{e}\}_{e\in W})|\chi_{\ell}(G_{W})|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}(\{g_{e}\}_{e\in W})\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\prod_{e\in W\setminus(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2})}\mathrm{d}h_{e}\,\underline{\langle\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{J_{\alpha},K_{\alpha}\}}(\{h_{e}\}_{e\in W\setminus(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2})},\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e\in T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}})|\chi_{\ell}(G_{W})}

\displaystyle\phantom{\int\prod_{e\in W\setminus(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2})}\mathrm{d}h_{e}\,}\underline{|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}(\{h_{e}\}_{e\in W\setminus(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2})},\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e\in T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}})\rangle}\,.

(78)

Let us break down this step: we start from eq.(
68
). Recall eq.(
3.2
), we can express the bulk-boundary map
|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}(\{g_{e}\}_{e\in W})\rangle
in terms of ’gluing operation’
\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\cup$\cr\hfil\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}\hfil\cr}}}
for bulk-boundary maps
|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{1},\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{(1)}\}}(\{g_{e^{\prime}}\}_{e^{\prime}\in T_{1}})\rangle
and
|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{2},\{j_{\alpha}^{(2)},k_{\alpha}^{(2)}\}}(\{g_{e^{\prime\prime}}\}_{e^{\prime\prime}\in T_{2}})\rangle
. We then gauge-fix the two bulk-boundary maps such that the holonomies along
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are gauge-fixed to
\mathbb{I}
in line with eq.(
23
), i.e.,

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}(\{g_{e}\}_{e\in W})\rangle

(82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[h_{e_{p}^{(1)}}\otimes h_{e_{q}^{(1)}}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{e^{\prime}\in\partial\Gamma_{1}\setminus(e_{p}^{(1)}\sqcup e_{q}^{(1)})}h_{v(e^{\prime})}^{\epsilon_{e^{\prime}}^{v}}\right)|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{1},\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{(1)}\}}(\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e^{\prime}\in T_{1}})\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\mathop{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\bigcup$\cr\hfil\raise 1.54999pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\times}$}\hfil\cr}}}\left[h_{e_{p}^{(2)}}\otimes h_{e_{q}^{(2)}}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{e^{\prime\prime}\in\partial\Gamma_{2}\setminus(e_{p}^{(2)}\sqcup e_{q}^{(2)})}h_{v(e^{\prime\prime})}^{\epsilon_{e^{\prime\prime}}^{v}}\right)|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{2},\{j_{\alpha}^{(2)},k_{\alpha}^{(2)}\}}(\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e^{\prime\prime}\in T_{2}})\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bigotimes_{e\in\partial\Gamma}h_{v(e)}^{\epsilon_{e}^{v}}\right)|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma,\{j_{\alpha},k_{\alpha}\}}(\{h_{e}\}_{e\in W\setminus(T_{1}\sqcup T_{2})},\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e\in T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}})\rangle\,.

The advantage with the gauge-fixing is that the action of loop holonomy operator is only nontrivial along piecewise edges
e_{p}^{(1)}
,
e_{q}^{(1)}
,
e_{q}^{(2)}
,
e_{p}^{(2)}
(interfacing edges between
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
), and is trivial along other piecewise edges. Notably, gauge-fixing does not change the loop holonomy, i.e.,

G_{W}=h_{e_{p}^{(2)}}\cdot h_{e_{q}^{(2)}}\cdot h_{e_{q}^{(1)}}\cdot h_{e_{p}^{(1)}}=G_{W_{(R)}}\,.

(83)

We put eq.(
82
) back to the eq.(
77
), and note that the boundary holonomies are erased by the scalar product ‘
\underline{\langle\,\ |\,\ \rangle}
’ for
{\mathcal{H}}_{\partial\Gamma}
due to
h^{\dagger}h=\mathbb{I}
, while the holonomies
h_{e_{p}^{(1)}}
,
h_{e_{p}^{(2)}}
,
h_{e_{q}^{(1)}}
,
h_{e_{q}^{(2)}}
are not erased by the ‘
\underline{\langle\,\ |\,\ \rangle}
’.

To handle eq.(
4.2
), we follow the spirit of eq.(
28
). We glue boundary edges along the
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
. The resulting intertwiners allow for decomposition:

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{1},\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{(1)}\}}(\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e^{\prime}\in T_{1}})\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{u_{1}}^{o}}\mathchoice{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Upsilon_{1}}^{\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(1)}\}}\,\Big]}^{{{I_{u_{1}}^{o}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.29207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56679pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Upsilon_{1}}^{\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(1)}\}}\,\Big]}^{{{I_{u_{1}}^{o}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.29207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56679pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Upsilon_{1}}^{\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(1)}\}}\,\Big]}^{{{I_{u_{1}}^{o}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.29207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56679pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{\Big[\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\Upsilon_{1}}^{\{j_{\alpha}^{(1)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(1)}\}}\,\Big]}^{{{I_{u_{1}}^{o}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.64325pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.29207pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56679pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}_{\{k_{\alpha}^{o(1)}\}}|\Psi_{\partial\Upsilon_{1},I_{u_{1}}}\rangle\,,

(84)

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\partial\Gamma_{2},\{j_{\alpha}^{(2)},k_{\alpha}^{(2)}\}}(\{\mathbb{I}\}_{e^{\prime\prime}\in T_{2}})\rangle=

Here
I_{u_{i}}=\{j_{\alpha}^{(i)},k_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}\cup I_{u_{i}}^{o}
and
\widetilde{I}_{u_{i}}=\{J_{\alpha}^{(i)},K_{\alpha}^{\partial(i)}\}\cup\widetilde{I}_{u_{i}}^{o}
.
The
\delta_{J_{\alpha}j_{\alpha}}
is imposed by scalar product ‘
\underline{\langle\,\ |\,\ \rangle}
’.
So eq.(
94
) actually represents the transition matrix on the coarse-grained graph
\Gamma_{(R)}
. Therefore, eq.(
78
) leads to the transformation for particular case
n=2
,

∎

Fig.
8
is a snapshot for the proof. The unitary
{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
for dual boundary Hilbert space is implemented by first gauge-fixing the holonomy along
e_{\alpha+1}
such that the holonomy operator acts trivially on
e_{\alpha+1}
, then gluing two bouquet edges and switching channel, thus the
e_{\alpha+1}
is coarse-grained during the action of holonomy operator.

A directly corollary of the Result
4.2
, is that we can compute expectation
\langle\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}\,\triangleright_{W}\rangle
from loopy spin network
Chen:2022rty
.

(96)

Every
{\mathcal{U}}_{\Upsilon_{i}}
is a unitary for internal space of intertwiner at
u_{i}
, thus they do not affect the spin-matching constraints between interfacing edges. Instead, they are to be interpreted as local unitaries at
u_{i}
, i.e., these unitaries do not affect spin entanglement. Indeed, following transformation eq.(
61
), the density matrix
\rho_{\partial\Gamma^{*}}
’s evolution is now given by

\displaystyle\rho_{\partial\Gamma^{*}}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\cdots{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}}^{\dagger}\,\rho_{\partial\Gamma_{(R)}^{*}}(t)\,{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}\cdots{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}}\,,

(97)

\displaystyle\rho_{\partial\Gamma_{(R)}^{*}}(t)

\displaystyle=

3pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell}}}}{\chi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\ell}}}}\,\triangleright_{W_{(R)}}}}}}\right]\,.

(98)

Partial tracing
\rho_{\Gamma}
over sub-networks
\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{i}
is equivalent to partial tracing
\rho_{\partial\Gamma^{*}}
over
({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}^{c}})^{*}
where
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}^{c}\equiv\partial(\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{i})
. It leads to the reduced density matrix for
({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}})^{*}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\partial\Gamma_{i}^{*}}(t)={\mathrm{Tr}}_{({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}^{c}})^{*}}\Big[\rho_{\partial\Gamma^{*}}(t)\Big]={\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\,\rho_{\partial\Upsilon_{i}^{*}}(t)\,{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}\,.

(99)

Therefore, the reduced density matrices
\rho_{\partial\Gamma_{i}^{*}}(t)
and
\rho_{\partial\Upsilon_{i}^{*}}(t)
are equivalent up to a unitary for dual boundary Hilbert space
({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}})^{*}
.

Result 4.3
.

Given a partition
\Gamma=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
, and an oriented loop
W
on
\Gamma
. Let the coarse-grained graph for
\Gamma
be
\Gamma_{(R)}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}\Upsilon_{i}
where
\Upsilon_{i}
are loopy graphs for respect
\Gamma_{i}
, and the coarse-grained loop
W_{(R)}
for
W
on
\Gamma_{(R)}
.
Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be any entanglement measure. Consider evolutions generated by loop holonomy operators
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}\,\triangleright_{W}
and the corresponding
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}\,\triangleright_{W_{(R)}}
.
Then the dynamics of spin network entanglement between
\Gamma_{i}
is identical to the dynamics of intertwiner entanglement between
\Upsilon_{i}
, i.e.,

{\mathcal{E}}[\rho_{\Gamma}(t)]={\mathcal{E}}[\rho_{\Gamma_{(R)}}(t)]\,.

(100)

k_{\alpha+2}
k_{\alpha+1}
k_{\alpha}
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathcal{B}}}\,\triangleright
k_{\alpha+2}
k_{\alpha}
\bullet
\bullet
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}

Figure 8
:
The illustration for unitary transformation on loop holonomy operator: in the left side one is able to gauge fix the holonomy associated with spin
k_{\alpha+1}
into
\mathbb{I}
, then implement gluing and channel transformation to coarse-grain
k_{\alpha+1}
.

At the end of the day, we have shown that spin network entanglement allows to be coarse-grained at dynamical level at least for the evolution generated by loop holonomy operator, thus one can study the dynamics of entanglement from coarse-grained graph.

5
Examples

We would like to conclude this paper with explicit examples of spin network entanglement coarse-graining, looking at entanglement excitation by loop holonomy operator.

5.1
Triangle graph

We consider the holonomy operator acting on the loop of triangle graph Fig.
9
. We compute explicitly the bipartite entanglement between
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
. Then we show that the bipartite entanglement can be studied from coarse-grained graph.

\bullet
C
j_{3}
\bullet
A
j_{1}
B
j_{2}
k_{1}
\bullet
k_{2}
\bullet
k_{3}
\bullet
\sum_{j_{23}}
j_{1}
j_{23}
j_{3}
\bullet
j_{2}
k_{3}
\bullet
k_{1}
\bullet
j_{1}
j_{3}
j_{2}
k_{3}
\bullet
k_{1}
\bullet
A
B^{\prime}

Figure 9
:
Coarse-graining triangle graph to candy graph.

Spin network states can involve superpositions of the bulk spins
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
,
K_{3}
while keeping boundary spins
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
,
j_{3}
. Such bulk spin superposition naturally induces a superposition of intertwiners. If this superposition carries correlations between the three vertices, this will be reflected in the entanglement between the four vertices.

Starting with an initial spin network basis state
|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle=|j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{2},k_{2},k_{1}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},k_{3},k_{2}\rangle_{C}
, we consider the evolution generated by the loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
,

\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{i-1}}\Big)\to\bigoplus_{\{K_{i}\}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{i-1}}\Big)\,.

For infinitesimal time
t\to 0
, the unitarity evolution operator is
e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}=\mathbb{I}-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}+{\mathcal{O}}(t^{2})
, which acts as

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}|j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{2},k_{2},k_{1}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},k_{3},k_{2}\rangle_{C}

(101)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{i}=|k_{i}-{\ell}|}^{k_{i}+{\ell}}\left(\delta^{K_{1}}_{k_{1}}\delta^{K_{2}}_{k_{2}}\delta^{K_{3}}_{k_{3}}-{\rm{i}}t{[}Z(tri)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\,\right)|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\phantom{\sum_{K_{i}=|k_{i}-{\ell}|}^{k_{i}+{\ell}}}\otimes|j_{2},K_{2},K_{1}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},K_{3},K_{2}\rangle_{C}+{\mathcal{O}}(t^{2})\,,

where
|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle_{A}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
,
|j_{2},K_{2},K_{1}\rangle_{B}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{B}
,
|j_{3},K_{3},K_{2}\rangle_{C}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
, denote the intertwiners living at respect trivalent vertex.
According to the general formula (
70
), the transition matrix
[Z(tri)_{\ell}]
is expressed in terms of
6j
-symbols for the action of loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}

\displaystyle{[}Z(tri)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{3}(j_{i}+k_{i}+K_{i}+\ell)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}

(102)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{3}\sqrt{(2k_{i}+1)(2K_{i}+1)}\,.

The matrix elements are all real numbers. We take special care in properly normalizing the truncated state,

|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle=\frac{|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{\{K_{i}\}}{[}Z(tri)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}{[}Z(tri)_{s}{]}^{\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}}}\,.

(103)

The normalization factor, at the denominator, can be computed explicitly using the composition rule of (
69
).
The normalization factor can then be evaluated to

\displaystyle N_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{3}(j_{i}+2k_{i}+s)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
s&k_{3}&k_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
s&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
s&k_{2}&k_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\prod_{i=1}^{3}(2k_{i}+1)

(104)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+t^{2}\sum_{\{K_{i}\}}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{3}(2k_{i}+1)(2K_{i}+1)\,.

(105)

We now look at the entanglement from the truncated state eq.(
131
). The initial spin network state is a basis state, thus is fully separable with vanishing entanglement. The final state is given by the density matrix
\rho_{tri}(t)=|\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle\langle\Psi_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)|
. Consider bipartition
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
. The reduced density matrix
\rho_{tri_{A}}(t)\in\textrm{End}({\mathcal{H}}_{A})
is obtained via partial tracing over
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
with orthonormal basis
|j_{2},K_{2},K_{1}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},K_{3},K_{2}\rangle_{C}\equiv|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}\rangle_{BC}
:

\displaystyle\rho_{tri_{A}}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}}\langle\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}|\rho_{tri}(t)|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}\rangle_{BC}

(106)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[t^{2}\sum_{K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}}\Big({[}Z(tri)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\Big)^{2}|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle\langle j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}|_{A}

\displaystyle\phantom{\bigg(}+|j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}\rangle\langle j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}|_{A}\bigg]\times\frac{1}{N_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}\,.

The eigenvalues of
\rho_{tri_{A}}(t)
can be read off directly from this formula since the reduced density matrix is diagonal in the
\big\{|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle\big\}
basis for
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rho_{tri_{A}}}[K_{1},K_{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{K_{1}}_{k_{1}}\,\delta^{K_{3}}_{k_{3}}+t^{2}\sum_{K_{2}}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\,\prod_{i=1}^{3}(2K_{i}+1)(2k_{i}+1)}{1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{3}(j_{i}+2k_{i}+s)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
s&k_{3}&k_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
s&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
s&k_{2}&k_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\prod_{i=1}^{3}(2k_{i}+1)}\,.

Now we look at the entanglement excitation on the coarse-grained graph. For bipartition
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
, we gauge fix
g_{2}\to\mathbb{I}
, then contract vertices
B,C
along
e_{2}
, acquiring a 4-valent vertices with spins
j_{2},j_{3},k_{1},k_{3}
as Fig.
9
. The spin network coarse-grained state from the triangle graph to the candy graph is given by

|\psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle=|j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}\rangle_{A}\otimes\sum_{j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}\,.

(108)

As initial state based on triangle graph, the initial state
|\psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle
based on the coarse-grained graph is a product state. The intertwiner at
B^{\prime}
is a superposition of basis states
|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}
with respect to internal spin
j_{23}
. The unitary matrix

{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}=(-1)^{2k_{2}+k_{3}-k_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}}\sqrt{(2k_{2}+1)(2j_{23}+1)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}

(109)

is account for the channel transformation, and the matrix elements are all real numbers, and the unitarity is verified by the orthogonality of
6j
-symbols,

\sum_{j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{\tilde{k}_{2},j_{23}}=\sum_{j_{23}}\sqrt{(2k_{2}+1)(2\tilde{k}_{2}+1)}(2j_{23}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&\tilde{k}_{2}\end{Bmatrix}=\delta_{k_{2}\tilde{k}_{2}}\,.

(110)

The unitarity requires the two
{\mathcal{U}}
having same
\{j,k\}
labeling since they are external labels for the intertwiner. On the other hand, the
k_{2},j_{23}
are internal labels for the intertwiner, so we also have

\sum_{k_{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},\widetilde{j}_{23}}=\delta_{j_{23}\widetilde{j}_{23}}\,.

(111)

The unitary
{\mathcal{U}}_{B^{\prime}}
links the transition matrices
Z(tri)
and
Z(tc)
,

{[}Z(tri)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}=\sum_{j_{23},J_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,K\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{K_{2},J_{23}}[Z(tc)_{\ell}]^{j_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{J_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\,,

(112)

where the transition matrix for
Z(tc)
is given by

\displaystyle[Z(tc)_{\ell}]^{J_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{J_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{j_{1}+j_{23}+K_{1}+k_{1}+K_{3}+k_{3}+2\ell}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{(2k_{1}+1)(2K_{1}+1)(2k_{3}+1)(2K_{3}+1)}\delta_{j_{1}J_{1}}\delta_{j_{23}J_{23}}\,.

(113)

Explicitly, the transformation is expressed in terms of
6j
-symbols:

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{3}(j_{i}+k_{i}+K_{i}+\ell)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{23}}(-1)^{2\ell+j_{1}+j_{23}+2K_{1}+2k_{3}+2k_{2}-2K_{2}}(2j_{23}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
K_{3}&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\phantom{\sum_{j_{23}}}\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\,.

(114)

As done in
Chen:2022rty
, this identity is related to Biedenharn-Elliot identity
Bonzom:2009zd
.

Let us compute the Schmidt eigenvalues from
|\psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle
. Similarly, starting with initial coarse-grained spin network state
|\psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle
as eq.(
108
), we consider the evolution generated by the loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}:

\displaystyle\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\Big)\otimes\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{K_{1},K_{3}}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{3}}\Big)\otimes\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{1}}\Big)\,.

Following the same logic as with the triangle graph, we compute the evolution of the state, truncated to leading order and properly normalized,

|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle=\frac{|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{K_{1},K_{3}}\sum_{j_{23}}[Z(tc)_{\ell}]^{j_{1},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}\sum_{j_{23}}[Z(tc)_{s}]^{j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}})^{2}}}\,.

(115)

Readers should notice that notation
|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle\equiv|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},K_{1},k_{2},K_{3}\}}\rangle
, i.e. the spin
k_{2}
is now fixed under the action of
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
on the candy graph.
The transition matrix is given by eq.(
113
), and the normalization factor
N_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)=N_{tri,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)
due to eq.(
114
).

Repeat the same procedure for entanglement
|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle
. The density matrix for final state on the coarse-grained graph is
\rho_{tc}(t)=|\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle\langle\Psi_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)|
. The reduced density matrix
\rho_{tc_{A}}(t)\in\textrm{End}({\mathcal{H}}_{A})
is obtained via partial tracing over
{\mathcal{H}}_{B^{\prime}}
, which can be done via choosing
\big\{|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}\big\}
orthonormal basis. So the reduced density matrix
\rho_{tc_{A}}(t)
reads:

\displaystyle\rho_{tc_{A}}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1},K_{3},j_{23}}\langle\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{3},K_{1}\},j_{23}|\rho_{tc}(t)|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{3},K_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}

(116)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(t^{2}\sum_{K_{1},K_{3},j_{23}}\Big([Z(tc)_{\ell}]^{j_{1},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{1},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{1},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle\langle j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}|_{A}

\displaystyle\phantom{\bigg(}+|j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}\rangle\langle j_{1},k_{1},k_{3}|_{A}\bigg)\times\frac{1}{N_{tc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}

where the
\Big({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}
is the probability distribution for
j_{23}
seen from another channel to the intertwiner
|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{3},k_{1}\},k_{2}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}
,

p(j_{23})=\Big({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}=(2k_{2}+1)(2j_{23}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\,.

(117)

Again, reduced density matrix (
116
) is a diagonal matrix with respect to
\{|j_{1},K_{1},K_{3}\rangle\}
basis for
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
, thus the eigenvalues of
\rho_{tc_{A}}(t)
are read

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rho_{tc_{A}}}[K_{1},K_{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(t^{2}\sum_{j_{23}}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&j_{2}&j_{3}\\
k_{2}&k_{3}&k_{1}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{3}(2k_{i}+1)

\displaystyle\phantom{\bigg(}\times(2K_{1}+1)(2K_{3}+1)(2j_{23}+1)+\delta^{K_{1}}_{k_{1}}\,\delta^{K_{3}}_{k_{3}}\bigg)\times\frac{1}{N_{tc,\{j_{i}\},\{k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}\,.

One can show
\lambda_{\rho_{tri_{A}}}[K_{1},K_{3}]=\lambda_{\rho_{tc_{A}}}[K_{1},K_{3}]
with eq.(
114
). Hence the entanglement excitation is preserved under the coarse-graining.

5.2
Square graph

We consider the holonomy operator acting on the loop of square graph Fig.
10
. We compute explicitly the bipartite entanglement between
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
where
\Gamma_{1}
is made of vertices
A,D
and their adjacent edges,
\Gamma_{2}
is made of vertices
B,C
and their adjacent edges. Then we show that the bipartite entanglement can be studied by its coarse-grained graph as Fig.
10
.

j_{1}
j_{2}
j_{3}
j_{4}
k_{1}
\bullet
k_{2}
\bullet
k_{3}
\bullet
k_{4}
\bullet
\sum_{j_{14},j_{23}}
j_{14}
j_{1}
\bullet
j_{4}
j_{23}
j_{2}
\bullet
j_{3}
k_{1}
\bullet
k_{3}
\bullet
j_{1}
j_{4}
j_{2}
j_{3}
k_{1}
\bullet
k_{3}
\bullet
A^{\prime}
B^{\prime}

Figure 10
:
Coarse-graining square graph to candy graph.

Starting with an initial spin network basis state
|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle=|j_{i},k_{4},k_{1}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{2},k_{1},k_{2}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},k_{2},k_{3}\rangle_{C}\otimes|j_{4},k_{3},k_{4}\rangle_{D}
, we consider the evolution generated by the loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
,

\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{i-1}}\Big)\to\bigoplus_{\{K_{i}\}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{4}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{i-1}}\Big)\,.

For infinitesimal time
t\to 0
, the unitarity evolution operator is
e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}=\mathbb{I}-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}+{\mathcal{O}}(t^{2})
, which acts as

\displaystyle e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle=e^{-{\rm{i}}t\,\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}}|j_{1},k_{1},k_{4}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{2},k_{2},k_{1}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},k_{3},k_{2}\rangle_{C}\otimes|j_{4},k_{4},k_{3}\rangle_{D}

(119)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{i}=|k_{i}-{\ell}|}^{k_{i}+{\ell}}\left(\delta^{K_{1}}_{k_{1}}\delta^{K_{2}}_{k_{2}}\delta^{K_{3}}_{k_{3}}\delta^{K_{4}}_{k_{4}}-{\rm{i}}t{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\right)|j_{1},K_{1},K_{4}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{2},K_{2},K_{1}\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\phantom{\sum_{K_{i}=|k_{i}-{\ell}|}^{k_{i}+{\ell}}}\otimes|j_{3},K_{3},K_{2}\rangle_{C}\otimes|j_{4},K_{4},K_{3}\rangle_{D}+{\mathcal{O}}(t^{2})\,,

where
|j_{i},K_{1},K_{4}\rangle_{A}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
,
|j_{2},K_{2},K_{1}\rangle_{B}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{B}
,
|j_{3},K_{3},K_{2}\rangle_{C}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
,
|j_{4},K_{4},K_{3}\rangle_{D}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{D}
denote the intertwiners living at respect trivalent vertex.
The transition matrix
[Z(squ)_{\ell}]
is expressed in terms of
6j
-symbols according to the general formula (
70
) for the action of loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}

\displaystyle{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}(j_{i}+k_{i}+K_{i}+\ell)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{4}\\
\ell&K_{4}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{4}&k_{4}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{4}\end{Bmatrix}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\sqrt{(2k_{i}+1)(2K_{i}+1)}\,.

(120)

Again, the matrix elements are all real numbers. Following the same logic as with the triangle graph, we compute the evolution of the state, truncated to leading order and properly normalized,

|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle=\frac{|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{\{K_{i}\}}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle}{\sqrt{1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}{[}Z(squ)_{s}{]}^{\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}}}\,,

(121)

and the normalization factor can then be evaluated to

\displaystyle N_{squ,\{j_{i}\},\{k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)=

\displaystyle 1+t^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}(j_{i}+2k_{i}+s)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{4}&k_{1}\\
s&k_{1}&k_{4}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{1}&k_{2}\\
s&k_{2}&k_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{2}&k_{3}\\
s&k_{3}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\phantom{1+}\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{4}&k_{3}&k_{4}\\
s&k_{4}&k_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\prod_{i=1}^{4}(2k_{i}+1)\,.

(122)

We now look at the reduced state from the pure state (
121
). The initial spin network state is a basis state, thus is fully separable with vanishing entanglement. The final state is given by density matrix
\rho_{squ}(t)=|\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle\langle\Psi_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)|
. The reduced density matrix
\rho_{squ_{AD}}\in\textrm{End}({\mathcal{H}}_{A}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{D})
is obtained via partial tracing over
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
, which is done via choosing orthonormal basis
|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}\rangle_{BC}\equiv|j_{2},K_{1},K_{2}\rangle_{B}\otimes|j_{3},K_{2},K_{3}\rangle_{C}
to implement
\sum_{K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}}\langle\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}|\rho_{squ}(t)|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\}\rangle_{BC}
, so the reduced density matrix
\rho_{squ_{AD}}(t)
reads:

\displaystyle\rho_{squ_{AD}}(t)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{squ,\{j_{i}\},\{k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}\bigg(|j_{1},k_{1},k_{4}\rangle\langle j_{1},k_{1},k_{4}|_{A}\otimes|j_{4},k_{4},k_{3}\rangle\langle j_{4},k_{4},k_{3}|_{D}

\displaystyle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{K_{4}}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},k_{r},K_{4}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},k_{r},K_{4}\}}\{j_{i},k_{r},k_{4}\}}|j_{1},k_{1},K_{4}\rangle\langle j_{1},k_{1},k_{4}|_{A}\otimes|j_{4},K_{4},k_{3}\rangle\langle j_{4},k_{4},k_{3}|_{D}

\displaystyle+{\rm{i}}t\sum_{K_{4}}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},k_{r},K_{4}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},k_{r},K_{4}\}}\{j_{i},k_{r},k_{4}\}}|j_{1},k_{1},k_{4}\rangle\langle j_{1},k_{1},K_{4}|_{A}\otimes|j_{4},k_{4},k_{3}\rangle\langle j_{4},K_{4},k_{3}|_{D}

\displaystyle+t^{2}\sum_{K_{4},K^{\prime}_{4}}\sum_{K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{r},K_{4}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{r},K_{4}\}}\{j_{i},k_{r},k_{4}\}}|j_{1},K_{1},K_{4}\rangle\langle j_{1},K_{1},K^{\prime}_{4}|_{A}

\displaystyle\phantom{+t^{2}\sum_{K_{4},K^{\prime}_{4}}\sum_{K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}}}\otimes|j_{4},K_{4},K_{3}\rangle\langle j_{4},K^{\prime}_{4},K_{3}|_{D}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{r},K^{\prime}_{4}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{r},K^{\prime}_{4}\}}\{j_{i},k_{r},k_{4}\}}\bigg)\,,

(123)

where
r=1,2,3
.

Now let us look at the reduced state from the point of view of coarse-graining. Considering bipartition
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{D}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
, we gauge fix
g_{2}\to\mathbb{I}
and
g_{4}\to\mathbb{I}
, then contract vertices
A,D
along
e_{4}
and vertices
B,C
along
e_{2}
, to acquire a two 4-valent vertices with respect spins
j_{1},j_{4},k_{1},k_{3}
and
j_{2},j_{3},k_{1},k_{3}
as Fig.
10
. The spin network coarse-grained state from the square graph to the candy graph is given by

|\psi_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle=\sum_{j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}|\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{1},k_{3}\},j_{14}\rangle_{A^{\prime}}\otimes\sum_{j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{2},k_{3}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}\,.

(124)

It has intertwiner superposition for
{\mathcal{H}}_{A^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B^{\prime}}
with respect to internal spins
j_{14}
and
j_{23}
.
The
|\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{1},k_{3}\},j_{14}\rangle_{A^{\prime}}
and
|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{2},k_{3}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}
are intertwiners for the two respect 4-valent vertices
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
, and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}
are unitaries for respect
{\mathcal{H}}_{A^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{2k_{4}+k_{1}-k_{3}+j_{1}+j_{4}}\sqrt{(2k_{4}+1)(2j_{14}+1)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{4}&j_{14}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{4}\end{Bmatrix}\,,

(125)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{2k_{2}+k_{3}-k_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}}\sqrt{(2k_{2}+1)(2j_{23}+1)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\,.

They are real numbers. The unitarity of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}
are verified by the orthogonality of
6j
-symbols,

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{\tilde{k}_{4},j_{14}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{14}}\sqrt{(2k_{4}+1)(2\tilde{k}_{4}+1)}(2j_{14}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{4}&j_{14}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{4}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{4}&j_{14}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&\tilde{k}_{4}\end{Bmatrix}=\delta_{k_{4}\tilde{k}_{4}}\,,

(126)

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{\tilde{k}_{2},j_{23}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{23}}\sqrt{(2k_{2}+1)(2\tilde{k}_{2}+1)}(2j_{23}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&\tilde{k}_{2}\end{Bmatrix}=\delta_{k_{2}\tilde{k}_{2}}\,.

As eq.(
111
), the unitarity requires the two
{\mathcal{U}}
having same
\{j,k\}
and for the internal labels,

\sum_{k_{4}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},\widetilde{j}_{14}}=\delta_{j_{14}\widetilde{j}_{14}}\,,\qquad\sum_{k_{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},\widetilde{j}_{23}}=\delta_{j_{23}\widetilde{j}_{23}}\,.

(127)

The unitaries
{\mathcal{U}}_{A^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{B^{\prime}}
link the transition matrices
Z(squ)
and
Z(sc)
,

{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}=\sum_{j_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,K\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{K_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,K\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{K_{2},j_{23}}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\,,

(128)

where the transition matrix for
Z(sc)
is given by

\displaystyle[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{J_{14},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{J_{14},J_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{j_{14}+j_{23}+K_{1}+k_{1}+K_{3}+k_{3}+2\ell}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{14}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{(2k_{1}+1)(2K_{1}+1)(2k_{3}+1)(2K_{3}+1)}\delta_{J_{14}j_{14}}\delta_{J_{23}j_{23}}\,,

(129)

or explicitly, the transformation is expressed as below identity in terms of
6j
-symbols:

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{4}(j_{i}+k_{i}+K_{i}+\ell)}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&k_{1}&k_{4}\\
\ell&K_{4}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&k_{2}&k_{1}\\
\ell&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{3}&k_{3}&k_{2}\\
\ell&K_{2}&K_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{4}&k_{4}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{4}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}(-1)^{j_{14}+j_{23}+k_{1}+K_{1}+k_{3}+K_{3}+2\ell+2k_{2}+2k_{4}-2K_{2}-2K_{4}}(2j_{14}+1)(2j_{23}+1)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{4}&j_{14}\\
K_{3}&K_{1}&K_{4}\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
K_{3}&K_{1}&K_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{14}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{23}&k_{1}&k_{3}\\
\ell&K_{3}&K_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{4}&j_{14}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{4}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}&j_{3}&j_{23}\\
k_{3}&k_{1}&k_{2}\end{Bmatrix}\,.

(130)

Let us look at the reduced state from the point of view of coarse-graining. For the coarse-grained state, the evolution is generated by the loop holonomy operator
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
along path
[A^{\prime}\overset{e_{3}}{\to}B^{\prime}\overset{e_{1}}{\to}A^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}:

\displaystyle\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{4}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{1}}\Big)\otimes\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{K_{1},K_{3}}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{4}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{1}}\Big)\otimes\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{1}}\Big)\,.

Following the same logic, the evolution of the state is truncated to leading order and properly normalized,

|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle=\frac{|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\rangle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{K_{1},K_{3}}\sum_{j_{14},j_{23}}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle}{\sqrt{N_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}}\,.

(131)

Note that here notation
|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\rangle\equiv|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},K_{1},k_{2},K_{3},k_{4}\}}\rangle
, because the spins
k_{2}
and
k_{4}
are fixed under the action of
\widehat{\chi_{\ell}}
on the candy graph.
The transition matrix is given by eq.(
129
). The normalization factor
N_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)=N_{squ,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)
is again due to eq.(
129
).

Repeat the same procedure. The density matrix for final state on the coarse-grained graph is
\rho_{sc}(t)=|\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)\rangle\langle\Psi_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)|
. The reduced density matrix
\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)\in\textrm{End}({\mathcal{H}}_{A^{\prime}})
is obtained via partial tracing over
{\mathcal{H}}_{B^{\prime}}
, which is done via choosing orthonormal basis
\big\{|\{j_{2},j_{3},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}\big\}
, so the reduced density matrix
\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)
reads:

\displaystyle\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1},K_{3},j_{23}}\langle\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{3},K_{1}\},j_{23}|\rho_{sc}(t)|\{j_{2},j_{3},K_{3},K_{1}\},j_{23}\rangle_{B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{sc,\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}(\ell,t)}\bigg(\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}|\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{14}\rangle\langle\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},\widetilde{j}_{14}|_{A^{\prime}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},\widetilde{j}_{14}}

\displaystyle-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}\Big({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},\widetilde{j}_{14}}

\displaystyle\phantom{-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}}|\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{14}\rangle\langle\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},\widetilde{j}_{14}|_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+{\rm{i}}t\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}\Big({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}_{\phantom{\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},\widetilde{j}_{14}}

\displaystyle\phantom{-{\rm{i}}t\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{j_{23}}}|\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{14}\rangle\langle\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},\widetilde{j}_{14}|_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+t^{2}\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{K_{1},K_{3},j_{23}}\Big({\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}\Big)^{2}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}\widetilde{j}_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}

\displaystyle\phantom{+t^{2}\sum_{j_{14},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\sum_{K_{1},K_{3},j_{23}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}|\{j_{1},j_{4},K_{3},K_{1}\},j_{14}\rangle\langle\{j_{1},j_{4},K_{3},K_{1}\},\widetilde{j}_{14}|_{A^{\prime}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},\widetilde{j}_{14}}\bigg)\,.

Now we show the eigenvalues of
\rho_{squ_{AD}}(t)
and
\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)
are identical.
With below alteration eq.(
128
)

[Z(sc)_{\ell}]^{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}_{\phantom{j_{14},j_{23},K_{1},K_{3}}j_{14},j_{23},k_{1},k_{3}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{k_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{k_{2},j_{23}}=\sum_{K_{2},K_{4}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,K\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{K_{4},j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,K\}_{B^{\prime}}}^{K_{2},j_{23}}{[}Z(squ)_{\ell}{]}^{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}_{\phantom{\{j_{i},K_{i}\}}\{j_{i},k_{i}\}}\,,

(132)

one can relate
\rho_{squ_{AD}}(t)
to
\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)
by

\rho_{squ_{AD}}(t)=M\rho_{sc_{A^{\prime}}}(t)M^{\dagger}

(133)

where
M
is a unitary map defined by

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{j_{4}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{K_{4}}\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{4}}\Big)\otimes\textrm{Inv}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}\Big({\mathcal{V}}_{j_{4}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{K_{4}}\otimes{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{3}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{4}}\sum_{j_{14}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\{j,k\}_{A^{\prime}}}^{K_{4},j_{14}}\Big(|j_{1},k_{1},K_{4}\rangle_{A}\otimes|j_{4},K_{4},k_{3}\rangle_{D}\Big)\langle\{j_{1},j_{4},k_{3},k_{1}\},j_{14}|_{A^{\prime}}\,.

(134)

Hence, the entanglement excitation between
A,D
and
B,C
is exactly reflected in the entanglement excitation between
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
.

5.3
Path-dependency on simplest two-loop graph

We consider the holonomy operator acting on the graph Fig.
11(a)
either along path
j_{1}\to k_{a}\to j_{2}
or path
j_{1}\to k_{b}\to j_{2}
. We look at the bipartite entanglement between
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
. We show the entanglement excitation’s dependency on the choices of path along which the holonomy operator acts. We also study the path-dependency from coarse-grained graph, converting the path-dependency to the spin-dependency of self-loop.

\bullet

(a)

Holonomy operator acts along path
j_{1}\to k_{a}\to j_{2}
or path
j_{1}\to k_{b}\to j_{2}
.

j_{3}

(b)

The coarse-grained graphs for path choices. Now the path dependency is reflected in the spin of self-loop.

Figure 11
:
The illustration for holonomy operator’s path-dependency. The path-dependency can be converted to spin-dependency via coarse-graining method.

For the sake of simplicity, we consider a simple spin network with spins
k_{a}=1,k_{b}=\frac{1}{2}
and
j_{1}=j_{2}=\frac{1}{2},j_{3}=1
, and set loop holonomy spin
\ell=\frac{1}{2}
.

Look at the action acting along the path
j_{1}\to k_{a}\to j_{2}
. The spin network state can be labeled by spin-shifting
|j_{1}j_{2}K_{1}\rangle_{a}
. The initial state is
|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}1\rangle_{a}
, and following same logic, the final truncated state reads,

|\psi_{a}(t)\rangle=\frac{|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}1\rangle_{a}+\frac{{\rm{i}}t}{2\sqrt{3}}|01\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{a}+\frac{{\rm{i}}t}{2\sqrt{3}}|10\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{a}+\frac{{\rm{i}}t}{3\sqrt{6}}|11\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{a}+\frac{4{\rm{i}}t}{3\sqrt{3}}|11\frac{3}{2}\rangle_{a}}{\sqrt{1+\frac{7t^{2}}{9}}}\,.

(135)

Likewise, look at the action acting along the path
j_{1}\to k_{b}\to j_{2}
. The spin network state can be labeled by spin-shifting
|j_{1}j_{2}K_{2}\rangle_{b}
. The initial state is
|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{b}
, and the final truncated state reads,

|\psi_{b}(t)\rangle=\frac{|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{b}+\frac{\sqrt{2}{\rm{i}}t}{3}|011\rangle_{b}+\frac{\sqrt{2}{\rm{i}}t}{3}|101\rangle_{b}+\frac{\sqrt{2}{\rm{i}}t}{3}|110\rangle_{b}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}{\rm{i}}t}{3\sqrt{3}}|111\rangle_{b}}{\sqrt{1+\frac{26t^{2}}{27}}}\,.

(136)

Partial tracing over
{\mathcal{H}}_{B}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
, the reduced states
{[}\rho_{a}(t){]}_{A}
and
{[}\rho_{b}(t){]}_{A}
are read,

\displaystyle{[}\rho_{a}(t){]}_{A}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\rangle\langle\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}|+\frac{t^{2}}{12}|01\rangle\langle 01|+\frac{t^{2}}{12}|10\rangle\langle 10|+\frac{11t^{2}}{18}|11\rangle\langle 11|}{1+\frac{7t^{2}}{9}}\,,

(137)

\displaystyle{[}\rho_{b}(t){]}_{A}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}\rangle\langle\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2}|+\frac{2t^{2}}{9}|01\rangle\langle 01|+\frac{2t^{2}}{9}|10\rangle\langle 10|+\frac{14t^{2}}{27}|11\rangle\langle 11|}{1+\frac{26t^{2}}{27}}\,.

(138)

The reduced states can be also derived from coarse-grained graph as Fig.
11(b)
. In this scenario, a loopy vertex affects the transition matrix through bouquet spin. In this sense, the loopy spin network in the problem amounts to being a 4-valent vertex. Based on that point, one can computes the eigenvalues (
) by setting the bouquet spin at
B^{\prime}
.
As expected, working on the coarse-grained graph leads to same reduced states. Interestingly, the path-dependency is translated into the spin-dependency on the self-loop. The different entanglement excitations are interpreted by the different transition matrices, and the different transition matrices are reflected in the different bouquet spins.

6
Conclusion & outlook

The present paper is dedicated to the study of coarse-graining spin entanglement at both kinematical and dynamical level in loop quantum gravity.
More precisely, we looked into the spin network entanglement on graph and coarse-grained graph, showing that arbitrary spin network state and the corresponding coarse-grained spin network state carry identical spin network entanglement. Furthermore, we show that the identical entanglement relation can be pushed towards the dynamical level as long as the evolution is generated by loop holonomy operator. That is, the dynamics of spin network entanglement can be also exactly reflected in the coarse-grained graph.

At the technical level, we utilize the point of view of bulk-boundary maps, understanding coarse-graining as unitary for dual boundary Hilbert space. This manner builds the relation between the spin network states based on graph and coarse-grained graph, guiding us to find the transformation between transition matrices for holonomy operator and the coarse-grained holonomy operator. Moreover, these unitaries are interpreted as local unitaries that preserves the entanglement amongst sub-networks. The conclusion is universal for any entanglement measure, according to the requirement that any entanglement measure should be invariant under local unitary.

We wish to shed light on the question about the quantum gravity degrees of freedom. In some sense, the coarse-graining feature of spin network entanglement implies that the graphical degrees of freedom can be considerably reduced.

On the other hand, the coarse-graining feature emphasizes the degrees of freedom of interfacing edges and self-loops. More precisely, the entanglement between spin sub-networks is exactly reflected in the entanglement between loopy spin sub-networks to which coarse-graining gives rise. Every loopy sub-network is made up by a single vertex plus boundary edges and self-loops (due to gauge-fixing). The bulk topology is reflected in the number of self-loops. When comes to spin network entanglement, the intertwiner at the single vertex encodes all the information about the sub-graph, associating with boundary spins and self-loop spins via recoupling.

Following the coarse-graining feature of spin network entanglement, we can consider a simple situation: staring with a closed spin network, partitioning it into a bipartite system, we then ask the maximal entanglement entropy between the subsystems. In line with our result, the entanglement entropy can be studied on the coarse-grained graph with two loopy vertices, and the maximal entanglement entropy is bounded from upper by the minor dimension of two loopy intertwiner spaces. Say
N
the number of interfacing edges, and
u
the loopy vertex with the number
L
of self-loops. Suppose that the intertwiner space associated with
u
has minor dimension, then the maximal entanglement entropy could be converted to a
\mathrm{U}(N+2L)
problem for computing dimension
Freidel:2009ck
. In particular, if one further assumes
BF
dynamics as considered in
Livine:2017xww
, then the self-loops could be removed at all, thus it becomes as 2-vertex model with
L=0

Borja:2010gn
;
Aranguren:2022nzn
. In this case, the maximal entanglement entropy could meet area-entropy law at certain limit
Freidel:2009ck
.

Since holonomy operators are the basic building blocks of the Hamiltonian dynamics of loop quantum gravity, this work gives a hint of non-local degrees of freedom description for the coarse-graining and dynamics on the quantum information carried by spin network states. We hope that further characterizing the various operators of loop quantum gravity through coarse-graining feature on the correlation and entanglement will allow to reformulate the precise mathematical framework for holography.
